# Decisions

What has been decided, by whom, and why. Claude Code adds build decisions at the bottom (the next ID in its lane, newest last). Anything in **Product decisions** needs the product lead's agreement to change.

**Status:** `Decided` — settled. `Default` — applied until the named owner confirms or overrides. `Open` — see `ARCHITECTURE.md` §11.

## IDs when several sessions build at once (BLD-A161)

**Build decisions carry a lane letter:** `BLD-A161`, `BLD-B230`, `BLD-C001`. Each lane counts on by itself, so lanes never have to agree on a number and there is no ceiling. Lanes A and B carry on from their last plain number. A new lane starts at 001.

**Old IDs stay as they are.** Plain numbers stopped at BLD-160 (lane A) and BLD-229 (lane B). Code, tests and commit messages cite those IDs.

**Product decisions keep one sequence** (DEC-096, DEC-097, and on), because they are the numbers the product lead refers to. Add a DEC row only on `main`, straight after `git pull --rebase`. Commit that file on its own and push at once.

**From a worktree, do this without touching the main checkout,** which other sessions share:

1. Commit your own work first.
2. Run `git fetch origin && git switch --detach origin/main`.
3. Add the row and commit.
4. Run `git push origin HEAD:main`.
5. Run `git switch -` and rebase.

Never run `git reset`, `git stash` or a whole-file `git add` in a checkout another session is using.

**A new session claims a lane** by taking the next free letter and adding a row below on `main` before it writes its first build decision.

**`make lint` checks all of this** (`scripts/ci/check_decision_ids.py`). It fails on any of these:

- an ID used twice;
- a malformed ID;
- an unregistered lane letter;
- a new plain-number build ID.

| Lane | Work | Where it works | Plain numbers it used | Migrations (BLD-A162) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Lead build: the gates, the demo stack, `main` | the main checkout and short-lived `.worktrees/*` branches | BLD-001 to BLD-160 | `202611211xxxxx` |
| B | Parallel build | `.worktrees/build-b*` | BLD-200 to BLD-229 | `202611212xxxxx` |
| C | Feature revisions and new features | `.worktrees/build-c` | none | `202611213xxxxx` |
| D | Design track: every screen and interaction | `.worktrees/design` | none | `202611214xxxxx` (reserved; it should not need one) |
| E | Planning and the design-handoff cut-over (`docs/SHIP_PLAN_1PC.md`): contracts, the schema cut-over, retirements, the idea page, My ideas, the Wall, Profile | `.worktrees/handoff` | none | `202611215xxxxx` (sorts after every other lane's, so the cut-over runs last on a fresh database) |
| F | The handoff's team-up, crew, conversation and search (`docs/SHIP_PLAN_1PC.md` §5) | `.worktrees/build-f*` | none | `202611226xxxxx` (after the cut-over) |
| G | Journeys: the end-to-end journey suite (`apps/web/journeys/`), journey walks at each checkpoint, breaks sent to the owning lane | `.worktrees/journeys` (branch `g/journeys`) | none | none (writes no migrations) |

**After the handoff cut-over (BLD-E006) every lane uses a range that sorts after it**, because the new tables arrive in the cut-over: A `202611221xxxxx`, B `202611222xxxxx`, C `202611223xxxxx`, D `202611224xxxxx`, E `202611225xxxxx` (the cut-over itself is `20261121500100`), F `202611226xxxxx`. Write no migration until the cut-over is on `main`; until then build against `1pc-handoff/06-contracts/`.

| DEC-004 | 6 Nov 2026 | **Category** is the submitter's call: cost saving, new revenue, both, not sure yet. **Play**: process improvement, upsell, cross-sell, new product, other | Committees sort first on value to KFintech; the submitter knows best | Decided | Product lead |
| DEC-005 | 6 Nov 2026 | The canvas is a **thinking aid**, not a form: plain questions, "why we ask", real examples, strength meter that guides and never blocks, assistant suggests and the person decides | People should ask themselves the right questions and come back to improve ideas | Decided | Product lead |
| DEC-006 | 6 Nov 2026 | **Attachments allowed** (up to 5 research files), plus references as links or notes on whether it is solved elsewhere and KFintech's advantage | Research already done saves weeks later | Decided | Product lead |

## Defaults awaiting confirmation (from ARCHITECTURE "Defaults applied")

| ID | Default | Confirm with |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Provider-neutral gateway; Anthropic API or AWS Bedrock, whichever InfoSec clears; zero data retention; agreed region | InfoSec |
| D2 | KFin private infrastructure, India region, containers | IT, InfoSec |
| D4 | Ideas visible org-wide; submitter can choose "committee only" with a reason | Product |
| D5 | Scorecard and priority weights as in `rules/`; 1% Club = accepted after presenting | Core committee, HR, Finance |
| D6 | Next.js + Postgres 16/pgvector + Python worker | Tech |
| D8 | Blind scoring | Core committee |
| D9 | People view for admins and committees only; each person sees their own | HR |

## Build defaults (Claude Code may revise; record the change below)

| ID | Default | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| BLD-001 | SQL migrations with `dbmate`; Kysely with generated types in TypeScript; psycopg 3 in Python | One schema source for two languages |
| BLD-002 | `LLM_MODE=fake` in tests (outputs from `agents/<name>/examples/`) | Tests never need a key or network |
| BLD-003 | `EMBEDDINGS_MODE=hash` in tests; BGE-M3 in UAT and prod | Fast deterministic tests |
| BLD-004 | Dev services: MinIO, ClamAV, a mock OIDC server, Mailpit | Local stack mirrors production roles |
| BLD-005 | One personal-data pattern file (YAML) loaded by both TypeScript and Python, with shared test vectors | Browser and server must agree |
| BLD-006 | Kill switches cached for 10 seconds | Fast to flip, cheap to check |

The full chain is not used: the core committee is senior, so a three-level chain would flag its members on most groups (in the synthetic directory the chair sits two levels above an e2e contributor). The "team named in who is affected" check is not used either: that section names kinds of people, not teams, and LOB committees are drawn from the LOB they review. An admin clears a manager flag with a reason on the new **Conflict flags** page (`/admin/conflicts`, linked from Admin). A contributor's own flag is never cleared; they step aside. If the chair has a conflict, an admin grants `core_chair` to a stand-in for that group. **Accepting opens the delivery handover** in the same transaction (lane A's `start_from_pick`, §4.8): build and reuse go to the build track, buy and partner to procurement, and the owner can change it in the pack. **A LOB with no active LOB committee** sends its round to the core committee (the cluster's route becomes `core`), so a group never waits on an empty roster. **Model calls leave the long transactions:** qualify starts in a `qualify.start` job after the approval commits, and an hourly `qualify.backfill` picks up groups approved while `committee.flow` was off or whose start failed. `prescore` and `pitch-prep` also run as jobs (`round.prescore`, `presentation.outline`), and opening a cycle takes an advisory lock. **Contributors read the pitch outline in neutral mode,** since it draws on every idea in the group (non-negotiable 7). **A no-show** gets its own message, `decision.not_presented` (new draft template `not-presented`, for HR review). Assessments close once the sheet is collated, and collation runs once. "Member n" follows a hash order, not submission order. Who offered to sponsor shows to the chair only after collation. Accepting needs a plain reason of at least 30 characters. A pass over the rules' "fail" needs a note. Scores, assessments and votes start unselected on screen. Changing the outcome clears the reason code. A decision on 29 February goes stale on 28 February a year later. Found while running the gates: committee emails failed to render (a local import shadowed `vault`); the decision sheet's score did not serialise; and the name detector read a cluster ID (`CL-0010`) as a person, so its tag reached the judge as a candidate ID and every scan that matched that cluster failed. The name detector now skips a value only when it is exactly one of the platform's own IDs; every other detection is unchanged. The e2e specs that flip `clusters.batch` take turns through a lock, and they find their group proposal by idea ID rather than by its words. Each fix has a regression test (9 new host tests) | Review findings; §3.1; §4.7 step 3 | The full manager chain (three levels); a deputy-chair role; holding LOB rounds until a committee exists | Yes |

A preliminary review's park or decline is now a decision on record (`decisions.round_id`), with its own decision file. **Filing:** the submitter or any co-contributor files once per decision from the new **Appeals** page (`/appeals`; message links lead there). They give grounds, a statement of up to 500 words and up to five web links. The links are stored as given and never fetched. They may also ask for a 10-minute hearing. Both personal-data checks run on the statement before anything is stored: the patterns, then the gatekeeper. If the gatekeeper is unavailable, the appeal waits rather than being stored unchecked. **Who hears it:** a LOB committee's decision goes to the whole core committee, minus anyone with a conflict. A core committee decision goes to a panel of three by rotation (fewest appeals heard first). The panel never includes anyone with a conflict on the group (the live §3.1 check) or the person who recorded the decision, and always includes at least one member who did not vote for the outcome. The most senior member chairs, taken as the longest on the committee because the HR export has no grade. If fewer than three qualify, every eligible member sits. **Outcomes** are recorded by the panel chair with at least 40 characters of reasons. Upheld returns the group to its outcome. Re-score sends it back to scoring, and the next round shows the panel's direction. Overturned sends a preliminary decision straight to an invitation, above the line; after a presentation, it returns the group to the chair to decide again, picked if capacity allows. The appeal file (`appeals/YYYY/APL-….md`, appellant as a contributor code, panel as a size) is linked from the decision file. `appeals/index.md` keeps live counts by deciding body and outcome, names removed. Messages `appeal.filed` (to the panel) and `appeal.decided` (to the colleagues) are new draft templates for HR. The not-shortlisted, not-accepted and not-presented drafts now say whether and until when the decision can be appealed. Behind a new switch, `appeals.filing` (off); the panel's decision is behind `committee.flow`. "Coming soon": appeals against review desk decisions (the day-one "Disagree with this?" stays) and against missed time limits, calendar invitations for the hearing, the full-committee re-hearing with the colleagues presenting, and the outside seat. | BLD-C012 | 11 Nov 2026 | **The worker's child process is a resource boundary, not a security boundary; outside dev and test the speech engine must run in an isolated service** (verification review of e95ef2e, new important finding). What holds: the child gets a cleared environment (the worker's database URL, keys and tokens are not passed), no inherited file descriptors, `python -I`, resource limits and a timeout. What does not: it runs as the worker's user on the worker's filesystem and network, so code that took control of a decoder (ffmpeg or PyAV inside faster-whisper) could read the worker's `/proc/<pid>/environ` or mounted secrets and reach Postgres and the store. The docstrings, ARCHITECTURE item 7, RUNBOOK and UAT now say exactly this, correcting BLD-C005's "never sees the database, keys or tokens". **Guard:** outside dev and test, `SPEECH_MODE=local` refuses with `isolation_required` unless `SPEECH_ENGINE_URL` points at the isolated speech service, which is built in round B (`engine_service_coming_soon` until then). The watch, every minute and at start, switches `capture.voice` off, audited, if it is on that way. The smoke check and the go/no-go row now pass only with the isolated service. The stdlib-only probe stays a child process (low risk) | A native codec exploit must never sit next to the database credentials and the encryption key | Seccomp, user namespaces or a separate uid inside the worker container: more fragile than a container of its own | Yes |

A WebM with more than 16 bytes after a sized Segment, or a second Segment anywhere, is refused. **Recorder:** "Record again" uses `aria-disabled` while the microphone prompt is open; a stop that fires during unmount does nothing; the playback URL is revoked on cleanup. **Mid-flight delete:** a note deleted before it became text ends as `failed` with the reason (`deleted_by_person`, `shared`…), and the person's status says so ("Your voice note is deleted."). **Drop failures:** when the store fails while dropping an idea's notes, it is counted and audited (`voice_note.drop_incomplete`, the idea's ID and the reason code; the bucket rule and the sweep finish the job) | Small gaps the review found | — | Yes |

The worker's repo writer was already polling. Between the seed's `git init` and its first commit, it read the half-seeded repo as a dirty tree from a crashed write, cleared git's lock files and reset it, so the seed's commit failed. A dev refresh of the seed files on a running stack had the same window. The seed now leaves a marker beside the repo (`.kfin-ideas.seeding`, in the folder the worker also mounts) while it writes, and waits 2 seconds for a writer round already under way. The writer skips its rounds while the marker is there or the repo has no commit yet, and checks again before each event. A marker older than 10 minutes is from a seed that died. After the independent review: the marker is created exclusively (two seeds cannot both hold it) and removed however the seed ends, an interrupt during the 2-second wait included; the writer warns when a seed holds it past a minute, and warns every round on a repo with no commit, which RUNBOOK §4.7 now explains | A launch-week CI run should fail only on a real fault | Stop the worker around `make seed` (slower, and CI would wait for it to come back); a file lock (`flock` does not reliably cross the Mac-to-VM file share) | Yes |
| BLD-C015 | 11 Nov 2026 | **The isolated speech service** (round B of the verification review; completes BLD-C012). A `speech` container under the compose profile `speech`, so dev and CI do not start it unless asked. Its image holds the speech engine (faster-whisper 1.1.1, with IndicConformer still to come) and one standard-library file, and nothing of the worker. It runs as uid 10002 with a read-only root filesystem, a 64 MB tmpfs `/tmp`, `cap_drop: ALL`, `no-new-privileges`, a pids limit, a memory limit (3 GB), no env file, no published port and the model mounted read-only. It sits on the `speech` network (`internal: true`), which only the worker shares: no route to Postgres, the store, mail, the web app or the internet. The worker sends the recording over HTTP with `X-Speech-Token` (a shared internal token given only to the worker and the service; in UAT it lives in the database env file, never the app env file) and gets text back. The service caps a request at 6 MB, gives each recording a wall-clock limit and keeps the model loaded between notes. Inside it, the engine runs in a long-lived child with a cleared environment (no token), address-space, core and file limits; a child that overruns or dies is killed and restarted, and that note fails. The worker refuses a `fake` engine outside dev and test, and retries while the service is unreachable. The deploy starts it when `SPEECH_ENGINE_URL` is set and checks the token and the model folder. `make uat-rehearsal` does not start it, because there is no model there to load | A decoder exploit must reach nothing worth having: no secret of the platform, no database and no internet | A separate uid, seccomp or user namespaces inside the worker container | Yes |

A compose test reads `infra/docker-compose.yml` and the UAT overlay and checks: the profile, read-only, tmpfs, non-root, no capabilities, no ports, no env file, the allowed environment only, read-only volumes, the internal network shared only by the worker and the service, and the web app never getting the token. Compose sets `WORKER_API_BIND_PEER=postgres`, a host only on the default network. At start the worker resolves it, finds its own interface on the same subnet (`core/netbind.py`) and listens there and on 127.0.0.1 (the container's health check) through pre-bound sockets, never on 0.0.0.0. The speech service, which reaches the worker only over the speech network, meets a closed port. If a peer is set but no interface matches, the worker refuses to start (`service.api_bind_refused`): binding everywhere would quietly reopen it. Without a peer (host-side runs, tests) it listens everywhere as before. | BLD-C018 | 11 Nov 2026 | **The speech server's token cannot be read by its engine child** (round B review, important 1, speech side). On Linux the server makes itself non-dumpable (`prctl(PR_SET_DUMPABLE, 0)`) first thing, so its `/proc/<pid>/environ` and `/proc/<pid>/mem` belong to root and the engine child, the same uid 10002, can neither read them nor attach to it. The server also takes the token out of its own environment once read. A test runs the real entry point and has the fake engine try `/proc/<parent>/environ`: "denied". A token in a mounted file was considered and not used: the child, as the same user, could read the file too. **Residual risk, accepted:** a kernel-level exploit from the engine could still reach the token. It is service-scoped: it only lets you send audio to this service for transcription, and the service reaches nothing else | The engine child is where an exploit would run | A file-mounted secret (readable by the same uid) | Yes |

A recording waits at most 30 s (`LOCK_WAIT_S`) for the engine, then gets 503 `busy`. The worker treats `busy` (and `engine_not_ready`) as retryable, so a note that only waited is tried again rather than failed for good. After the wait, a recording whose client has gone (the connection closed) is not run. The worker's client timeout is the engine's limit plus the wait plus 15 s, always above the service's own | A long recording must not make every other note fail | A queue with a position, which is more than one service needs | Yes |

An engine answer over 1 MB is refused and the engine restarted. `_start` kills a child that hung or failed while starting. The child speaks on a duplicate of fd 1, and fd 1 itself goes to /dev/null before the model loads. The service needs a token of 32 or more characters (the dev default is 32). | BLD-C112 | 17 Nov 2026 | **Drafts by voice stream through the worker, and nothing is stored as audio** (DEC-108 (1), (2); `L-DRAFT.voice`; J-SHARE/3 "By voice", J-RESUME/1). `POST /api/drafts/:id/voice/start`, `/chunk?stream=&seq=` and `/end`, the sharer only (`submit`), behind `capture.voice`, `speech.hosted` and `submissions`; `GET /api/drafts/:id/voice/:stream` reads a note. The browser sends raw 16 kHz mono 16-bit PCM, about a second (32,000 bytes) a chunk, at most 2 seconds, numbered from 0; **Sarvam's streaming API accepts only WAV or PCM, so S-04 captures with an AudioWorklet, not MediaRecorder's WebM/Opus**. The worker holds the stream (the browser never holds the key) and relays each chunk as base64 as it arrives: **the audio is never stored, decoded or opened, nothing is written to the store, so there is no file scan step** (non-negotiable 8 has no file to apply to). The 3-minute cap is counted exactly from the bytes; `voice.max_per_hour` counts voice notes and streams together; a retried chunk is answered without being sent twice; a person's other live stream ends first (`replaced`); no chunk for 20 seconds ends the note and keeps what was heard (J-RESUME/1); `speech.hosted` is read fresh before each chunk goes out, and if it or `capture.voice` has gone off the note ends (`paused`) with the words kept. **Polling, not server-sent events:** `A-API.events` does not exist yet, so each chunk's answer carries the words heard so far (their own, with `spans`) and the GET polls. At the end the words are normalised (spoken numbers to digits), masked (`hide_text`), and the masked copy translated by mayura:v1 into English and masked again. `voice_streams` (worker only, pinned) holds the unmasked words sealed with the vault key, the masked copy and the English copy until `voice_to_message(conn, stream_id, idea_uuid=, by_code=)` hands them to the draft once (`text` own words, `text_masked`, `spans`, `text_en`, `text_en_label` "Typed up in English", `lang`, `language`, `seconds`, `summary` "Voice message, 1 minute 12 seconds, in Telugu"), or `voice.sweep` deletes them after 24 hours. A failed translation keeps the person's words (`text_en` null, `translation_failure`). This also narrows, but cannot close, the gap between the proxy's and the route handlers' kill-switch reads: they are separate bundles, each with its own 10-second cache, so `/api/status` can list a switch a moment after the proxy first refused. That is not an ordering bug in the session code; `test_b1_08` polls `/api/status` for up to 15 seconds after the 503.
